# Proposed OEIS comments: A298914 to A302638

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A298914

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A298914>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [6858](../papers/6501-7000/6858-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = a(n-1) +a(n-2) +a(n-3) +a(n-5) -a(n-6) -a(n-7) -a(n-8) for n>9.

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 1 024 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 9. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A298967

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A298967>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [172](../papers/0001-0500/0172-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 75 (see link above).

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 1 024 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 75, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A300212

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A300212>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [149](../papers/0001-0500/0149-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 89 (see link above).

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 1 024 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 89, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A301921

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A301921>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [45](../papers/0001-0500/0045-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: Let k be a positive integer. The sequence obtained by reducing a(n) modulo k is eventually periodic with the period dividing phi(k) = A000010(k). For example, modulo 9 we obtain the sequence [0, 1, 3, 1, 6, 4, 0, 7, 3, 1, 6, 4, 0, 7, 3, 1, 6, 4, 0, 7, ...] with an apparent period of 6 = phi(9) beginning at n = 2. Cf. A004123. (End)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This is true. Under t = e^x - 1 the entry's e.g.f. becomes an ordinary power series with integer coefficients; that integrality kills all but finitely many terms of the Stirling expansion of a(n) modulo k, and what is left is an integer combination of the sequences n -> j^n, each eventually periodic mod k with period dividing phi(k). - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
